class="infobox-label">Currency</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Euro" title="Euro">Euro</a> (€) (<a href="/wiki/ISO_4217" title="ISO 4217">EUR</a>)</td></tr><tr class="mergedtoprow"><th href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><div class="hlist"><ul><li><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:BL" title="ISO 3166-2:BL">BL</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:FR" title="ISO class="infobox-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><div class="hlist"><ul><li><a href="/wiki/.bl" title=".bl">.bl</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/.fr" title=".fr">.fr</a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Saint Barthélemy</b> (<a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">Saint-Barthélemy</i>, <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="fr-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/French" title="Help:IPA/French">[sɛ̃ baʁtelemi]</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/2\/2a\/LL-Q150_%28fra%29-Xenoph%C3%B4n-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.wav\/LL-Q150_%28fra%29-Xenoph%C3%B4n-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.wav.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;LL-Q150 (fra)-Xenoph\u00f4n-Saint-Barth\u00e9lemy.wav&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/2/2a/LL-Q150_%28fra%29-Xenoph%C3%B4n-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.wav/LL-Q150_%28fra%29-Xenoph%C3%B4n-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.wav.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:LL-Q150_(fra)-Xenoph%C3%B4n-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.wav" title="File:LL-Q150 (fra)-Xenophôn-Saint-Barthélemy.wav">i</a></sup>), officially the <b>Collectivité territoriale de Saint-Barthélemy</b>,<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> also known as <b>St. Barts</b> (English)<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> or <b>St. Barth</b> (French), is an <a href="/wiki/Overseas_collectivity" title="Overseas collectivity">overseas collectivity</a> of <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a><sup id="cite_ref-Government_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Government-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> in the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a>. The island lies about 30 kilometres (19&#160;mi) southeast of the island of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Martin_(island)" title="Saint Martin (island)">Saint Martin</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> it is northeast of the Dutch islands of <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">Sint Eustatius</a>, as well as north of the independent country of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Kitts_and_Nevis" title="Saint Kitts and Nevis">Saint Kitts and Nevis</a>.
</p><p>Saint Barthélemy was for many years a French commune forming part of <a href="/wiki/Guadeloupe" title="Guadeloupe">Guadeloupe</a>, which is an overseas region and department of France. In 2003 the island voted in favour of <a href="/wiki/Secession" title="Secession">secession</a> from Guadeloupe to form a separate <a href="/wiki/Overseas_collectivity" title="Overseas collectivity">overseas collectivity</a> (<i>collectivité d'outre-mer</i>, abbreviated to <i>COM</i>) of France. The collectivity is one of four territories among the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands" title="Leeward Islands">Leeward Islands</a> in the northeastern <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> that make up the <a href="/wiki/French_West_Indies" title="French West Indies">French West Indies</a>, along with <a href="/wiki/Collectivity_of_Saint_Martin" title="Collectivity of Saint Martin">Saint Martin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guadeloupe" title="Guadeloupe">Guadeloupe</a> (200 kilometres (120&#160;mi) southeast), and <a href="/wiki/Martinique" title="Martinique">Martinique</a>.
</p><p>Saint Barthélemy, a volcanic island fully encircled by shallow reefs, has an area of 25 square kilometres (9.7&#160;sq&#160;mi)<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;note 3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-area_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-area-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> and a population of 9,961 at the Jan. 2017 census.<sup id="cite_ref-census_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Its <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital</a> is <a href="/wiki/Gustavia,_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Gustavia, Saint Barthélemy">Gustavia</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-cia_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-13">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> which also contains the main harbour. It is the only Caribbean island that was a <a href="/wiki/Swedish_colony_of_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Swedish colony of Saint Barthélemy">Swedish colony</a> for any significant length of time. It remained so for nearly a century before it returned to French rule <a href="/wiki/1877_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_status_referendum" title="1877 Saint Barthélemy status referendum">after a referendum</a>. Symbolism from the <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Sweden" title="Coat of arms of Sweden">Swedish national arms</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Three_Crowns" title="Three Crowns">Three Crowns</a>, still appears in the island's coat of arms. The language, cuisine, and culture, however, are distinctly <a href="/wiki/French_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="French culture">French</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The island is a popular tourist destination during the winter holiday season, geared towards the high-end, luxury tourist market.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The island was named by <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Columbus" title="Christopher Columbus">Christopher Columbus</a> for his younger brother <a href="/wiki/Bartholomew_Columbus" title="Bartholomew Columbus">Bartholomew Columbus</a> in 1493.<sup id="cite_ref-Government_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Government-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/French_West_Indies" title="French West Indies">French West Indies</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_period">Early period</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Early period">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Before European contact the island was possibly frequented by Eastern Caribbean <a href="/wiki/Ta%C3%ADno" title="Taíno">Taíno</a> and <a href="/wiki/Arawak" title="Arawak">Arawak</a> people, who called the island 'Ouanalao',<sup id="cite_ref-wimco_villa_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wimco_villa-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> though it is believed that the island was not inhabited permanently due to its poor water sources and soil.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;note 4&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Columbus" title="Christopher Columbus">Christopher Columbus</a> was the first European to encounter the island in 1493.<sup id="cite_ref-wimco_villa_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wimco_villa-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Sporadic visits continued for the next hundred years until formal colonisation began taking shape.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-13">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="17th_century">17th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: 17th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>By 1648 the island was settled by the French, encouraged by <a href="/wiki/Phillippe_de_Longvilliers_de_Poincy" title="Phillippe de Longvilliers de Poincy">Phillippe de Longvilliers de Poincy</a>, the lieutenant-governor of the French West India Company, and initially comprised about 50 to 60 settlers, later augmented by smaller numbers coming from <a href="/wiki/St_Kitts" class="mw-redirect" title="St Kitts">St Kitts</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Maher_article_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Maher_article-17">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Led by Jacques Gentes, the new arrivals began cultivating <a href="/wiki/Theobroma_cacao" title="Theobroma cacao">cacao</a>.  However, the settlement was attacked by Caribs in 1656 and briefly abandoned.<sup id="cite_ref-wimco_villa_14-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wimco_villa-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Luthin_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Luthin-18">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>De Poincy was the dominant administrator in this period and a member of the <a href="/wiki/Knights_Hospitaller" title="Knights Hospitaller">Order of Saint John</a>. He facilitated the transfer of ownership from the <a href="/wiki/Compagnie_des_%C3%8Eles_de_l%27Am%C3%A9rique" class="mw-redirect" title="Compagnie des Îles de l&#39;Amérique">Compagnie des Îles de l'Amérique</a> to the Order. He continued to rule the island until he died in 1660. Five years later, it was bought by the <a href="/wiki/French_West_India_Company" title="French West India Company">French West India Company</a> along with the Order's other possessions in the Caribbean.<sup id="cite_ref-wimco_villa_14-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wimco_villa-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> By 1674, the company was dissolved and the islands became part of the French Kingdom and added to the colony of <a href="/wiki/Guadeloupe" title="Guadeloupe">Guadeloupe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Luthin_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Luthin-18">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="18th_century">18th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: 18th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Swedish_colony_of_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Swedish colony of Saint Barthélemy">Swedish colony of Saint Barthélemy</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:St%C3%A4mpel_f%C3%B6r_guvern%C3%B6r_%C3%B6ver_den_svenska_kolonin_S-t_Barth%C3%A9lemy,_V%C3%A4stindien,_1784-1877_-_Livrustkammaren_-_102551.tif" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/St%C3%A4mpel_f%C3%B6r_guvern%C3%B6r_%C3%B6ver_den_svenska_kolonin_S-t_Barth%C3%A9lemy%2C_V%C3%A4stindien%2C_1784-1877_-_Livrustkammaren_-_102551.tif/lossy-page1-170px-St%C3%A4mpel_f%C3%B6r_guvern%C3%B6r_%C3%B6ver_den_svenska_kolonin_S-t_Barth%C3%A9lemy%2C_V%C3%A4stindien%2C_1784-1877_-_Livrustkammaren_-_102551.tif.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="191" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/St%C3%A4mpel_f%C3%B6r_guvern%C3%B6r_%C3%B6ver_den_svenska_kolonin_S-t_Barth%C3%A9lemy%2C_V%C3%A4stindien%2C_1784-1877_-_Livrustkammaren_-_102551.tif/lossy-page1-255px-St%C3%A4mpel_f%C3%B6r_guvern%C3%B6r_%C3%B6ver_den_svenska_kolonin_S-t_Barth%C3%A9lemy%2C_V%C3%A4stindien%2C_1784-1877_-_Livrustkammaren_-_102551.tif.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/St%C3%A4mpel_f%C3%B6r_guvern%C3%B6r_%C3%B6ver_den_svenska_kolonin_S-t_Barth%C3%A9lemy%2C_V%C3%A4stindien%2C_1784-1877_-_Livrustkammaren_-_102551.tif/lossy-page1-340px-St%C3%A4mpel_f%C3%B6r_guvern%C3%B6r_%C3%B6ver_den_svenska_kolonin_S-t_Barth%C3%A9lemy%2C_V%C3%A4stindien%2C_1784-1877_-_Livrustkammaren_-_102551.tif.jpg 2x" data-file-width="635" data-file-height="712" /></a><figcaption>Seal of the governor of the Swedish colony, 1784–1877</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:StBart_quartiers_1801.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/StBart_quartiers_1801.jpg/290px-StBart_quartiers_1801.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="219" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/StBart_quartiers_1801.jpg/435px-StBart_quartiers_1801.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/StBart_quartiers_1801.jpg/580px-StBart_quartiers_1801.jpg 2x" data-file-width="9474" data-file-height="7142" /></a><figcaption>Historical <i>quartiers</i> (1801)</figcaption></figure>
<p>The island proved economically unsuccessful, and was subject to the activities of pirates (most notably <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Montbars" title="Daniel Montbars">Daniel Montbars</a> aka 'Montbars the Exterminator'), as well as <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Great_Britain" title="Kingdom of Great Britain">the British</a>, who attacked the island in 1744.<sup id="cite_ref-wimco_villa_14-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wimco_villa-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Thus deeming it to be of little worth, <a href="/wiki/King_Louis_XVI" class="mw-redirect" title="King Louis XVI">King Louis XVI</a> traded the island to <a href="/wiki/Sweden" title="Sweden">Sweden</a> in 1784 in return for trading privileges in <a href="/wiki/Gothenburg" title="Gothenburg">Gothenburg</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DD_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DD-19">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> This change of control saw progress and prosperity as the Swedes declared <a href="/wiki/Gustavia,_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Gustavia, Saint Barthélemy">Gustavia</a> (named after the Swedish king <a href="/wiki/Gustav_III" title="Gustav III">Gustav III</a> who ruled at that time) a free port, convenient for trading by the Europeans for goods, including <a href="/wiki/Contraband" title="Contraband">contraband</a> material.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p._22-23_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p._22-23-20">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-wimco_villa_14-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wimco_villa-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="19th_century">19th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: 19th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Slavery was practised in St. Barthélemy under the <i>Ordinance concerning the Police of Slaves and free Coloured People</i><sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> of 1787. The last legally owned slaves in the Swedish colony of St. Barthélemy were granted their freedom by the state on 9 October 1847.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Since the island was not a plantation area, the freed slaves suffered economic hardships due to lack of opportunities for employment.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p._24_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p._24-23">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In 1852, a devastating hurricane<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> hit the island and this was followed by a fire.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The economy suffered, and thus Sweden sought to relieve themselves of the island. In 1867, a volcano “nearly destroyed the island” as recorded in the Illustrated London News.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Following <a href="/wiki/1877_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_status_referendum" title="1877 Saint Barthélemy status referendum">a referendum</a> in 1877, Sweden sold the island back to France in 1878,<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_pp._157-159_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_pp._157-159-24">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> after which it was administered as part of <a href="/wiki/Guadeloupe" title="Guadeloupe">Guadeloupe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DD_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DD-19">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Swedish media supported the sale of the island to France, characterizing the island's poverty as a source of national humiliation for Sweden.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Barth_-_Vue_a%C3%A9rienne_-_Octobre_2009.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Saint_Barth_-_Vue_a%C3%A9rienne_-_Octobre_2009.jpg/290px-Saint_Barth_-_Vue_a%C3%A9rienne_-_Octobre_2009.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="156" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Saint_Barth_-_Vue_a%C3%A9rienne_-_Octobre_2009.jpg/435px-Saint_Barth_-_Vue_a%C3%A9rienne_-_Octobre_2009.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Saint_Barth_-_Vue_a%C3%A9rienne_-_Octobre_2009.jpg/580px-Saint_Barth_-_Vue_a%C3%A9rienne_-_Octobre_2009.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3590" data-file-height="1935" /></a><figcaption>View of St. Barthélemy</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="20th_century">20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: 20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 19 March 1946, the people of the island became French citizens with full rights.<sup id="cite_ref-wimco_villa_14-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wimco_villa-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
With few economic prospects on the islands, many men from St. Barthélemy took jobs on <a href="/wiki/Saint_Thomas,_U.S._Virgin_Islands" title="Saint Thomas, U.S. Virgin Islands">Saint Thomas</a> to support their families.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Organised tourism and hotels began in earnest in 
the 1960s and developed in the 1970s onwards, particularly after the building of the island's landing strip that can accommodate mid-sized aircraft.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The island soon became renowned as a high-class luxury destination, being frequented by numerous celebrities such as <a href="/wiki/Greta_Garbo" title="Greta Garbo">Greta Garbo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Howard_Hughes" title="Howard Hughes">Howard Hughes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_de_Rothschild" title="Benjamin de Rothschild">Benjamin de Rothschild</a>, <a href="/wiki/David_Rockefeller" title="David Rockefeller">David Rockefeller</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lorne_Michaels" title="Lorne Michaels">Lorne Michaels</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chevy_Chase" title="Chevy Chase">Chevy Chase</a>, <a href="/wiki/Steve_Martin" title="Steve Martin">Steve Martin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jimmy_Buffett" title="Jimmy Buffett">Jimmy Buffett</a> and <a href="/wiki/Johnny_Hallyday" title="Johnny Hallyday">Johnny Hallyday</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-wimco_villa_14-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wimco_villa-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The boost in tourist numbers has led to a rise in living standards and rapid modernisation.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The island was not <a href="/wiki/Electrification" title="Electrification">electrified</a> until the 1980s.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="21st_century">21st century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: 21st century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Saint Barthélemy was for many years a French commune forming part of <a href="/wiki/Guadeloupe" title="Guadeloupe">Guadeloupe</a>, which is an overseas region and department of France. Through a <a href="/wiki/2003_Guadeloupean_autonomy_referendum" title="2003 Guadeloupean autonomy referendum">referendum in 2003</a>, island residents sought separation from the administrative jurisdiction of Guadeloupe, and it was finally accomplished in 2007.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The island of Saint Barthélemy became an Overseas Collectivity (COM). A governing territorial council was <a href="/wiki/2007_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Territorial_Council_election" title="2007 Saint Barthélemy Territorial Council election">elected</a> for its administration, which has provided the island with a certain degree of autonomy. A senator represents the island in <a href="/wiki/Paris" title="Paris">Paris</a>. St. Barthélemy has retained its free port status.<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Introduction_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Introduction-28">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
Saint Barthélemy ceased being an outermost region and <a href="/wiki/Withdrawal_from_the_European_Union#Saint_Barth.C3.A9lemy" title="Withdrawal from the European Union">left the EU</a>, to become an OCT, (Overseas Country or Territory) on 1 January 2012.
</p><p>The island sustained damage from <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Irma" title="Hurricane Irma">Hurricane Irma</a> in September 2017 but recovered quickly, and by early 2018 transport and electricity were largely operational.<sup id="cite_ref-wimco_villa_14-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wimco_villa-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:SSS_Islands_Map.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/SSS_Islands_Map.png/280px-SSS_Islands_Map.png" decoding="async" width="280" height="360" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/SSS_Islands_Map.png/420px-SSS_Islands_Map.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f1/SSS_Islands_Map.png 2x" data-file-width="527" data-file-height="678" /></a><figcaption>Map showing the location of St. Barts relative to <a href="/wiki/Saint_Martin_(island)" title="Saint Martin (island)">Sint Maarten/Saint Martin</a> and <a href="/wiki/St_Kitts" class="mw-redirect" title="St Kitts">St Kitts</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_Island_topographic_map-fr.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_Island_topographic_map-fr.svg/330px-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_Island_topographic_map-fr.svg.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_Island_topographic_map-fr.svg/495px-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_Island_topographic_map-fr.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_Island_topographic_map-fr.svg/660px-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_Island_topographic_map-fr.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="2430" data-file-height="1620" /></a><figcaption>Map of Saint-Barthélemy</figcaption></figure>
<p>Approximately 250 kilometres (160&#160;mi) east of <a href="/wiki/Puerto_Rico" title="Puerto Rico">Puerto Rico</a> and the nearer <a href="/wiki/Virgin_Islands" title="Virgin Islands">Virgin Islands</a>, St. Barthélemy lies immediately southeast of the islands of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Martin_(island)" title="Saint Martin (island)">Saint Martin</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anguilla" title="Anguilla">Anguilla</a>. St. Barthélemy is separated from Saint Martin by the <a href="/wiki/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_Channel" title="Saint-Barthélemy Channel">Saint-Barthélemy Channel</a>. It lies northeast of <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a> and <a href="/wiki/St_Eustatius" class="mw-redirect" title="St Eustatius">St Eustatius</a>, and north of <a href="/wiki/St_Kitts" class="mw-redirect" title="St Kitts">St Kitts</a>. Several smaller uninhabited islands lie offshore, the largest of which are <a href="/wiki/%C3%8Ele_Fourchue" title="Île Fourchue">Île Fourchue</a>, Île Coco, <a href="/wiki/%C3%8Ele_Chevreau" title="Île Chevreau">Île Chevreau</a> (Île Bonhomme), <a href="/wiki/%C3%8Ele_Fr%C3%A9gate" title="Île Frégate">Île Frégate</a>, <a href="/wiki/%C3%8Ele_Toc_Vers" title="Île Toc Vers">Île Toc Vers</a>, <a href="/wiki/La_Tortue_ou_l%27Ecalle" class="mw-redirect" title="La Tortue ou l&#39;Ecalle">Île Tortue</a>, Roche Plate (Table à Diable) and Mancel ou la Poule et les Poussins. There are numerous smaller islets, such as La Petite Islette, L'Îlet au Vent, Île Pelé, Île le Boulanger, Roche le Bœuf, Île Petit Jean, L'Âne Rouge, Les Gros Islets, La Baleine des Gros Islets, Pain de Sucre, Les Baleines du Pain de Sucre, Fourmis, Les Petit Saints, Roches Roubes, Les Baleines de Grand Fond and Les Grenadins.<sup id="cite_ref-Barnett1876_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barnett1876-29">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Marine_areas">Marine areas</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Marine areas">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>St. Barthélemy forms, with St. Martin, Anguilla, and <a href="/wiki/Dog_Island,_Anguilla" title="Dog Island, Anguilla">Dog Island</a>, a distinct group that lies upon the western edge of a flat bank of soundings composed chiefly of shells, sand, and coral. From St. Barthélemy, the bank extends east-southeast, ending in a small tongue or spit. It is separated from the main bank by a narrow length of deep water. East of the island, the edge of the bank lies 22 kilometres (14 miles) away.<sup id="cite_ref-Barnett1876_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barnett1876-29">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Grande Saline Bay provides temporary anchorage for small vessels while Colombier Bay, to the northwest, has a 4 fathoms patch near mid-entrance. In the bight of St. Jean Bay, there is a narrow cut through the reef.<sup id="cite_ref-Barnett1876_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Barnett1876-29">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> The north and east sides of the island are fringed, to a short distance from the shore, by a visible coral reef. Reefs are mostly in shallow waters and are clearly visible. The coastal areas abound with beaches and many of these have offshore reefs, some of which are part of a marine reserve.<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The marine reserve, founded in 1999, covers more than 1,000 hectares (2,500 acres) of protected and vulnerable habitats, bays, and islands, and includes a zone that is restricted to scientific observations only.<sup id="cite_ref-SpeightHenderson2010_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SpeightHenderson2010-30">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> As the sea surrounding the St. Barthélemy is rich in coral reefs and other precious marine life, the area has been declared a protected area since 1996. Environmental awareness is quite pronounced in St. Barthélemy and is promoted by the Environmental Commission.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan-31">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Gustavia_San_Barth_001.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Gustavia_San_Barth_001.jpg/220px-Gustavia_San_Barth_001.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Gustavia_San_Barth_001.jpg/330px-Gustavia_San_Barth_001.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Gustavia_San_Barth_001.jpg/440px-Gustavia_San_Barth_001.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1544" data-file-height="1026" /></a><figcaption>View of <a href="/wiki/Gustavia,_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Gustavia, Saint Barthélemy">Gustavia</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Shell_Beach_St._Barts.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Shell_Beach_St._Barts.jpg/220px-Shell_Beach_St._Barts.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Shell_Beach_St._Barts.jpg/330px-Shell_Beach_St._Barts.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Shell_Beach_St._Barts.jpg/440px-Shell_Beach_St._Barts.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="480" /></a><figcaption>Shell Beach (Anse De Grand Galet)</figcaption></figure>
<p>There are as many as 22 public beaches (most beaches on St. Barthélémy are known as "Anse de...") of which 15 are considered suitable for swimming.  They are categorized and divided into two groups, the leeward side (calm waters protected by the island itself) and the windward side (some of which are protected by hills and reefs). Windward beaches are popular for windsurfing. The beach of St Jean is suitable for water sports and facilities have been created for that purpose. The long beach at Lorient has shade and is a quiet beach as compared to St. Jean.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.177-178_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.177-178-32">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Grand-cul-de-sac is a long beach with facilities for water sports. Anse de Flamands is a very wide sandy beach and Le petit Anse (The little beach), just to the north of Anse de Flamands is very safe and popular with the locals for their children. Anse Toiny beach is in a remote location and is considered suitable for experienced surfers as the water current is very strong.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.177-178_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.177-178-32">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On the leeward side, the notable beaches are Anse du Gouverneur, Anse du Colombier which is only accessible by foot or by boat, Anse de Grand Galet (Shell Beach), and Anse de Grande Saline which is popular with nudists. The area around the salt ponds near the Anse de Grande Saline beach is marshy and is a habitat for tropical birds. Ile islet, an offshoot of the leeward side, has a white sandy beach.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.177-178_32-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.177-178-32">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Shell Beach, also called Anse de Grand Galet (in French, 'Anse' means "cove" and Galet means "pebble"), is a beach in the southwestern part of Gustavia. A large number of sea shells are scattered on this beach. This beach was subject to the strong waves of hurricane <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Lenny" title="Hurricane Lenny">Lenny</a> in 1999, which resulted in the erosion of the sand. This necessitated supplementing the beach with new sand in 2000.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.170-173_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.170-173-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On the north coast, on the far eastern side of the island, there are two lagoons called the Anse de Marigot<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> and Anse du Grand Cul-de-Sac.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.170-173_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.170-173-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Beach_at_the_Anse_de_Grande_Saline_on_St._Barthelemy,_French_West_Indies_-_panoramio.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Beach_at_the_Anse_de_Grande_Saline_on_St._Barthelemy%2C_French_West_Indies_-_panoramio.jpg/220px-Beach_at_the_Anse_de_Grande_Saline_on_St._Barthelemy%2C_French_West_Indies_-_panoramio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="139" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Beach_at_the_Anse_de_Grande_Saline_on_St._Barthelemy%2C_French_West_Indies_-_panoramio.jpg/330px-Beach_at_the_Anse_de_Grande_Saline_on_St._Barthelemy%2C_French_West_Indies_-_panoramio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Beach_at_the_Anse_de_Grande_Saline_on_St._Barthelemy%2C_French_West_Indies_-_panoramio.jpg/440px-Beach_at_the_Anse_de_Grande_Saline_on_St._Barthelemy%2C_French_West_Indies_-_panoramio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1724" data-file-height="1091" /></a><figcaption>Beach at the Anse de Grande Saline</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Interior_areas">Interior areas</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Interior areas">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Morne_de_Vitet" title="Morne de Vitet">Morne de Vitet</a>, 286 metres (938 feet) in height, is the highest peak on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Government_10-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Government-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> Hills and valleys of varying topography cover the rest of the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Notable are Morne Rouge, Morne Criquet, Morne de Grand Fond, Morne de Dépoudré and Morne Lurin. The largest bodies of water on the island are Étang de Saint-Jean, Grande Saline, Grand Étang, and Petit Étang.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Populated_areas">Populated areas</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Populated areas">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The population is spread among 40 <i>quartiers</i>, roughly corresponding to settlements. They are grouped into two <i>paroisses</i> (parishes):
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_quartiers_map.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_quartiers_map.svg/300px-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_quartiers_map.svg.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="201" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_quartiers_map.svg/450px-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_quartiers_map.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_quartiers_map.svg/600px-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_quartiers_map.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1275" data-file-height="855" /></a><figcaption>Territorial subdivisions into two <i>paroisses</i> (parishes) with 40 <i>quartiers</i></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_(ISS-018).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_%28ISS-018%29.jpg/220px-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_%28ISS-018%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="244" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_%28ISS-018%29.jpg/330px-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_%28ISS-018%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_%28ISS-018%29.jpg/440px-Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy_%28ISS-018%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2988" data-file-height="3320" /></a><figcaption>Satellite picture of the island</figcaption></figure>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The island covers an area of 25 square kilometres (10&#160;sq&#160;mi). The eastern side is wetter than the western. Although the climate is essentially arid, the rainfall does average 1,000 millimetres (40 inches) annually, but with considerable variation over the terrain. Summer is from May to November, which is also the rainy season. Winter from December to April is the dry season. Sunshine is very prominent for nearly the entire year and even during the rainy season. Humidity, however, is not very high due to the winds. The average temperature is around 25&#160;°C (77&#160;°F) with day temperatures rising to 32&#160;°C (90&#160;°F). The average high and low temperatures in January are 28&#160;°C (82&#160;°F) and 22&#160;°C (72&#160;°F), respectively, while in July they are 30&#160;°C (86&#160;°F) and 24&#160;°C (75&#160;°F). The lowest night temperature recorded is 13&#160;°C (55&#160;°F). The Caribbean sea waters in the vicinity generally maintain a temperature of about 27&#160;°C (81&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.4_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.4-34">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>As of 2017, Saint-Barthélemy had a population of 9,961.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;note 5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-census_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Residents, known as Saint-Barthélemois, are French citizens. Most of them are descendants of the first settlers, of <a href="/wiki/Brittany" title="Brittany">Breton</a>, <a href="/wiki/Normandy" title="Normandy">Norman</a>, <a href="/wiki/Poitou" title="Poitou">Poitevin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Saintonge_(region)" title="Saintonge (region)">Saintongeais</a> and <a href="/wiki/Duchy_of_Anjou" title="Duchy of Anjou">Angevin</a> lineage. There is also a big community of Portuguese emigrants mainly from the North of Portugal, around 3000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a> is the native tongue of the population, though English is understood in most hotels and restaurants; a small population of <a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">Anglophones</a> has been resident in Gustavia for many years.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The St. Barthélemy French <a href="/wiki/Patois" title="Patois">patois</a> is spoken by some 500–700 people in the leeward portion of the island and is superficially related to <a href="/wiki/Quebec_French" title="Quebec French">Quebec French</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-CalvetBrown2006_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CalvetBrown2006-37">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Valdman1997_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Valdman1997-38">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> whereas <a href="/wiki/French-based_creole_languages" title="French-based creole languages">Créole French</a> is limited to the windward side. Unlike other populations in the Caribbean, language preference between the Créole and Patois is geographically, and not racially, determined.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="This citation requires a reference to the specific page or range of pages in which the material appears. (January 2011)">page&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Structure_of_the_population">Structure of the population</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Structure of the population">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1127642053">.mw-parser-output .hidden-begin{box-sizing:border-box;width:100%;padding:5px;border:none;font-size:95%}.mw-parser-output .hidden-title{font-weight:bold;line-height:1.6;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .hidden-content{text-align:left}</style><div class="hidden-begin mw-collapsible mw-collapsed" style=""><div class="hidden-title" style="background:#EEBC35;">Population by Sex and Age Group (Census 01.I.2015): <sup id="cite_ref-auto_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-44">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup></div><div class="hidden-content mw-collapsible-content" style="">
<table class="wikitable">

<tbody><tr>
<th width="80pt">Age Group
</th>
<th width="80pt">Male

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:St_Barths_Gustavia_Eglise_Catholique_1.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/St_Barths_Gustavia_Eglise_Catholique_1.JPG/220px-St_Barths_Gustavia_Eglise_Catholique_1.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/St_Barths_Gustavia_Eglise_Catholique_1.JPG/330px-St_Barths_Gustavia_Eglise_Catholique_1.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/St_Barths_Gustavia_Eglise_Catholique_1.JPG/440px-St_Barths_Gustavia_Eglise_Catholique_1.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3872" data-file-height="2592" /></a><figcaption>Church of Our Lady of the Assumption, Gustavia (<i>Église Notre-Dame-de-l'Assomption de Gustavia</i>)</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:St_Barths_Lorient_Eglise_1.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/St_Barths_Lorient_Eglise_1.JPG/220px-St_Barths_Lorient_Eglise_1.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/St_Barths_Lorient_Eglise_1.JPG/330px-St_Barths_Lorient_Eglise_1.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d1/St_Barths_Lorient_Eglise_1.JPG/440px-St_Barths_Lorient_Eglise_1.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3872" data-file-height="2592" /></a><figcaption>Church of Our Lady of the Assumption, Lorient (<i><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%89glise_Notre-Dame_de_l%27Assomption,_Lorient&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Église Notre-Dame de l&#39;Assomption, Lorient (page does not exist)">Église Notre-Dame-de-l'Assomption de Lorient</a></i>)</figcaption></figure>
<p>The majority of the population of Saint Barthélemy is <a href="/wiki/Christian" class="mw-redirect" title="Christian">Christian</a>; Saint Barthélemy is considered the most religiously homogeneous<sup id="cite_ref-:0_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-45">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> territory in the French West Indies, with particular importance given to the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-45">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The territory of Saint-Barthélemy forms the parish of <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Our Lady of the Assumption</a> (<i>Notre-Dame-de-l'Assomption</i>), which in turn depends on the diocese of Basse-Terre and Pointe-à-Pitre ( <i>diocèse de Basse-Terre et Pointe-à-Pitre</i>) whose see is located in the <a href="/wiki/Basse-Terre_Cathedral" title="Basse-Terre Cathedral">cathedral of Our Lady of Guadeloupe</a> (<i>Notre-Dame-de-Guadeloupe</i>).
</p><p>The presbytery is located in the district of Lorient and dates from 1822. The building, including the <a href="/wiki/Masonry" title="Masonry">masonry</a> terrace, the <a href="/wiki/Stairs" title="Stairs">staircases</a>, the outbuildings and the <a href="/wiki/Garden" title="Garden">gardens</a> have been protected as historic monuments of France since March 28, 2002.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are two main Catholic churches both dedicated to Our Lady of the Assumption or Notre-Dame-de-l'Assomption (<a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%89glise_Notre-Dame_de_l%27Assomption,_Lorient&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Église Notre-Dame de l&#39;Assomption, Lorient (page does not exist)">the one in Lorient</a><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> and the one in Gustavia)<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> and a <a href="/wiki/Chapel" title="Chapel">chapel</a> of <a href="/wiki/Catherine_of_Siena" title="Catherine of Siena">St. Catherine of Siena</a> (<i>Chapelle de Sainte Catherine de Sienne</i>)<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> in Colombier.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Saint Barthélemy is also part of the Diocese of the North Eastern Caribbean and <a href="/wiki/Aruba" title="Aruba">Aruba</a> (<i>diocèse de la Caraïbe du Nord-Est et d'Aruba</i>), which is under the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> (<a href="/wiki/Church_in_the_Province_of_the_West_Indies" title="Church in the Province of the West Indies">Church in the Province of the West Indies</a>) that serves a small minority on the island that is centered in the Anglican Church of Saint Bartholomew (<i>Église anglicane de Saint-Barthélemy</i>) built between 1853 and 1855.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The small <a href="/wiki/Pentecostalism" title="Pentecostalism">Pentecostal</a> Christian community does not have a church building of its own and therefore offers religious services at St Barth's Beach Hotel.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The main religious holidays are <a href="/wiki/Christmas" title="Christmas">Christmas</a>, the day of the <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Assumption</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Mary,_mother_of_Jesus" title="Mary, mother of Jesus">Virgin Mary</a> (August 15), <a href="/wiki/All_Saints%27_Day" title="All Saints&#39; Day">All Saints' Day</a> (November 1) and the feast of St. Bartholomew (patron saint of the island) on August 24.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Politics and government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
