data-file-height="1187" /></a></span></th></tr><tr><th style="border-bottom: #aaa 1px solid"></th></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content" style="text-align:left; border-bottom: 1px #aaa solid;">
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Territorial_Council_of_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Territorial Council of Saint Barthélemy">Territorial Council</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="List of political parties in Saint Barthélemy">Political parties</a></li>
<li><a class="mw-selflink selflink">Elections</a>: <a href="/wiki/2007_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Territorial_Council_election" title="2007 Saint Barthélemy Territorial Council election">2007</a>, <a href="/wiki/2012_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Territorial_Council_election" title="2012 Saint Barthélemy Territorial Council election">2012</a>, <a href="/wiki/2017_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Territorial_Council_election" title="2017 Saint Barthélemy Territorial Council election">2017</a>, <a href="/wiki/2022_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Territorial_Council_election" title="2022 Saint Barthélemy Territorial Council election">2022</a></li></ul></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-below">
<div class="hlist" style="text-align:center;font-weight:normal;">
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Outline_of_political_science#Politics_by_region" title="Outline of political science">Other countries</a></li></ul>
</div></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Politics_of_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Template:Politics of Saint Barthélemy"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Politics_of_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Template talk:Politics of Saint Barthélemy"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Politics_of_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Until 2007 the whole island of St. Barthélemy was a French <a href="/wiki/Commune_in_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Commune in France">commune</a> (<i>commune de Saint-Barthélemy</i>), forming part of <a href="/wiki/Guadeloupe" title="Guadeloupe">Guadeloupe</a> which is an <a href="/wiki/R%C3%A9gion_d%27outre-mer" class="mw-redirect" title="Région d&#39;outre-mer">overseas <i>région</i></a> and <a href="/wiki/D%C3%A9partement_d%27outre-mer" class="mw-redirect" title="Département d&#39;outre-mer">overseas <i>département</i></a> of France. In 2003, the population voted through a referendum in favour of secession from Guadeloupe to form a separate <a href="/wiki/Collectivit%C3%A9_d%27outre-mer" class="mw-redirect" title="Collectivité d&#39;outre-mer">overseas collectivity</a> (<i>collectivité d'outre-mer</i>, or <i>COM</i>) of France.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 7 February 2007,<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/French_Parliament" title="French Parliament">French Parliament</a> passed a bill granting COM status to both St. Barthélemy and (separately) to the neighbouring <a href="/wiki/Collectivity_of_Saint_Martin" title="Collectivity of Saint Martin">Saint Martin</a>. The new status took effect on 15 July 2007, when the first <a href="/wiki/Territorial_Council_of_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Territorial Council of Saint Barthélemy">territorial council</a> was elected, according to the law.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> The island has a president (elected every five years), a <a href="/wiki/Unicameral" class="mw-redirect" title="Unicameral">unicameral</a> Territorial Council of nineteen members who are elected by popular vote and serve for five-year terms, and an executive council of seven members. Elections to these councils were first held on 1 July 2007 with the most recent election in 2022.
</p><p>One senator represents the island in the <a href="/wiki/Senate_(France)" title="Senate (France)">Senate</a>, while a deputy jointly elected with Saint Martin represents it in the <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_(France)" title="National Assembly (France)">National Assembly</a>. St. Barthélemy became an <a href="/wiki/Special_member_state_territories_and_the_European_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Special member state territories and the European Union">overseas territory of the European Union</a> on 1 January 2012,<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> but the island's inhabitants remain French citizens with EU status holding EU passports. France is responsible for the defence of the island and as such has stationed a security force on the island comprising six policemen and thirteen <a href="/wiki/Gendarmerie" title="Gendarmerie">gendarmes</a> (posted on a two-year term).<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The French State is represented by a <a href="/wiki/Prefect_(France)" title="Prefect (France)">Prefect</a> appointed by the President on the advice of the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_the_Interior_(France)" title="Minister of the Interior (France)">Minister of the Interior</a>. As a collectivity of France, the island's national anthem is <i>La Marseillaise</i>, though <i><a href="/wiki/L%27Hymne_a_St._Barth%C3%A9lemy" class="mw-redirect" title="L&#39;Hymne a St. Barthélemy">L'Hymne a St. Barthélemy</a></i> is also used unofficially.<sup id="cite_ref-Government_10-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Government-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_France" title="Economy of France">Economy of France</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Barts_yachts.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/St._Barts_yachts.jpg/220px-St._Barts_yachts.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/St._Barts_yachts.jpg/330px-St._Barts_yachts.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/St._Barts_yachts.jpg/440px-St._Barts_yachts.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Sailboat" title="Sailboat">Sailboats</a> and <a href="/wiki/Yacht" title="Yacht">yachts</a> in St. Barts</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a><figcaption>A proportional representation of Saint Barthélemy exports, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<p>The economy of the island is based on tourism and duty-free retail.<sup id="cite_ref-Economy_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economy-57">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The official currency of St. Barthélemy is the <a href="/wiki/Euro" title="Euro">euro</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Economy_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economy-57">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It is estimated that the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal)">nominal GDP</a> of Saint Martin amounted to 367&#160;million euros in 2014 (US$487&#160;million at 2014 exchanges rates; US$411&#160;million at Feb. 2022 exchange rates).<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> In that same year the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal) per capita">nominal GDP per capita</a> of Saint Barthelemy was 38,994 euros (US$51,735 at 2014 exchanges rates; US$43,626 at Feb. 2022 exchange rates),<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_7-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> which was one of the highest GDP per capita in the Caribbean, more than double the GDP per capita of the nearby <a href="/wiki/Collectivity_of_Saint_Martin" title="Collectivity of Saint Martin">Collectivity of Saint Martin</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> as well as 85% higher than <a href="/wiki/Guadeloupe" title="Guadeloupe">Guadeloupe</a> and 19% higher than <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_France" title="Metropolitan France">metropolitan France</a>'s GDP per capita in 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>International investment and the wealth generated by tourists explain the high standard of living on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Economy_57-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economy-57">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Most of the food is imported from the United States or France.<sup id="cite_ref-Economy_57-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economy-57">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Tourism attracts about 200,000 visitors every year.<sup id="cite_ref-Economy_57-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economy-57">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> As a result, there is a boom in house-building activity catering to the tourists and also to the permanent residents of the island.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>St. Barthélemy has about 25 hotels, most with 15 rooms or fewer; the largest has 58 rooms.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Hotels are classified in the traditional French manner; 3 Star, 4 Star and 4 Star Luxe. Of particular note are <a href="/wiki/Eden_Rock,_St_Barths" title="Eden Rock, St Barths">Eden Rock</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cheval_Blanc,_St_Barths" title="Cheval Blanc, St Barths">Cheval Blanc</a>. <a href="/wiki/Hotel_Le_Toiny" title="Hotel Le Toiny">Hotel Le Toiny</a>, the most expensive hotel on the island, has 12 rooms.
</p><p>Most places of accommodation are in the form of private villas, of which there are some 400 available to rent on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> The island's tourism industry, though expensive, attracts 70,000 visitors every year to its hotels and villas; another 130,000 people arrive by boat. It also attracts a labour force from <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Economy_57-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Economy-57">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> A team of analysts have analysed <a href="/wiki/Airbnb" title="Airbnb">Airbnb</a>’s Luxe offerings in 27 of their most popular luxury locations around the world and concluded that St Barths in the Caribbean is the top location for luxury Airbnb accommodation worldwide.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Corossol" title="Corossol">Corossol</a> is noted for its handicrafts; weaving hats and bags from palm fronds is a low-income economic activity of the indigenous people.<sup id="cite_ref-Cameron(2007)_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cameron(2007)-61">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Wildlife">Wildlife</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Wildlife">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/R%C3%A9serve_naturelle_nationale_de_Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy" class="mw-redirect" title="Réserve naturelle nationale de Saint-Barthélemy">Réserve naturelle nationale de Saint-Barthélemy</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora">Flora</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Flora">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_vegetation.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_vegetation.jpg/220px-St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_vegetation.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_vegetation.jpg/330px-St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_vegetation.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_vegetation.jpg/440px-St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_vegetation.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption>Vegetation at <a href="/w/index.php?title=Baie_de_Saint-Jean&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Baie de Saint-Jean (page does not exist)">Baie de Saint-Jean</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>As the terrain is generally arid, the hills have mostly poor soil and support only <a href="/wiki/Cacti" class="mw-redirect" title="Cacti">cacti</a> and <a href="/wiki/Succulent" class="mw-redirect" title="Succulent">succulent</a> plants. During the rainy season, the area turns green with vegetation and grass. The eastern part of the island is greener as it receives more rainfall. A 1994 survey has revealed several hundred indigenous species of plants including the naturalized varieties of flora; some grow in irrigated areas while the dry areas are dominated by the cacti variety. <a href="/wiki/Coccoloba_uvifera" title="Coccoloba uvifera">Sea grapes</a> and <a href="/wiki/Palm_tree" class="mw-redirect" title="Palm tree">palm trees</a> are a common sight with <a href="/wiki/Mangrove" title="Mangrove">mangroves</a> and <a href="/wiki/Shrub" title="Shrub">shrubs</a> surviving in the saline coastal swamps. <a href="/wiki/Coconut_palm" class="mw-redirect" title="Coconut palm">Coconut palm</a> was brought to the island from the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Pacific islands">Pacific islands</a>. Important plants noted on the island include <a href="/wiki/Delonix_regia" title="Delonix regia">flamboyant trees</a>, <a href="/wiki/Plumeria" title="Plumeria">frangipanis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sabal" title="Sabal">sabal palms</a>, <a href="/wiki/Poui" class="mw-redirect" title="Poui">wild trumpet</a> and <a href="/wiki/Manchineel" title="Manchineel">Manchineel trees</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.7_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.7-62">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Other trees of note include the <a href="/wiki/Royal_palm" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal palm">royal palm</a>, sea grape trees in the form of shrubs on the beaches, and as 5 to 7 metres (16 to 23 feet) trees in the interior areas of the island, <a href="/wiki/Aloe" title="Aloe">aloe</a> or <a href="/wiki/Aloe_vera" title="Aloe vera">aloe vera</a> (brought from the <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean" class="mw-redirect" title="Mediterranean">Mediterranean</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.8_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.8-63">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/Night_blooming_cereus" class="mw-redirect" title="Night blooming cereus">night blooming cereus</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mammillaria" title="Mammillaria">mamillaria nivosa</a>, yellow prickly pear or <a href="/wiki/Barbary_fig" class="mw-redirect" title="Barbary fig">barbary fig</a> which was planted as barbed wire defences against invading <a href="/wiki/British_army" class="mw-redirect" title="British army">British army</a> in 1773, Mexican <a href="/wiki/Cactus" title="Cactus">cactus</a>, <a href="/wiki/Stapelia_gigantea" title="Stapelia gigantea">stapelia gigantea</a>, <a href="/wiki/Golden_trumpet" class="mw-redirect" title="Golden trumpet">golden trumpet</a> or <a href="/wiki/Yellow_bell" title="Yellow bell">yellow bell</a> which was originally from South America, <a href="/wiki/Bougainvillea" title="Bougainvillea">bougainvillea</a> and others.<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.9_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.9-64">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.10_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.10-65">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Fauna">Fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Marine mammals are many, such as <a href="/wiki/Dolphins" class="mw-redirect" title="Dolphins">dolphins</a>, <a href="/wiki/Porpoises" class="mw-redirect" title="Porpoises">porpoises</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Whales" class="mw-redirect" title="Whales">whales</a>, are seen here during the migration period from December until May. <a href="/wiki/Turtle" title="Turtle">Turtles</a> are a common sight along the coastline of the island. They are a protected species and in the endangered list. It is stated that it will take 15–50 years for this species to attain reproductive age. Though they live in the sea, the females come to the shore to lay eggs and are protected by private societies. Three species of turtles are particularly notable. These are: The <a href="/wiki/Leatherback_sea_turtle" title="Leatherback sea turtle">leatherback sea turtles</a> which have leather skin instead of a shell and are the largest of the type found here, sometimes measuring as much as 3 metres (10 feet) (average is about 1.5&#160;m or 5&#160;ft) and weighing about 450 (<a href="/wiki/Jellyfish" title="Jellyfish">jellyfish</a> is their favourite diet); the <a href="/wiki/Hawksbill_turtles" class="mw-redirect" title="Hawksbill turtles">hawksbill turtles</a>, which have hawk-like beaks and found near reefs, generally about 90 centimetres (35 inches) in diameter and weigh about 60 and their diet consists of <a href="/wiki/Crabs" class="mw-redirect" title="Crabs">crabs</a> and <a href="/wiki/Snails" class="mw-redirect" title="Snails">snails</a>; and the <a href="/wiki/Green_turtles" class="mw-redirect" title="Green turtles">green turtles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Herbivores" class="mw-redirect" title="Herbivores">herbivores</a> which have rounded heads, generally about 90 centimetres (35 inches) in diameter and live amidst tall sea grasses.<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.11-12_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.11-12-66">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Avifauna">Avifauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Avifauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Male_Frigate_bird.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Male_Frigate_bird.jpg/170px-Male_Frigate_bird.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="256" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Male_Frigate_bird.jpg/255px-Male_Frigate_bird.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Male_Frigate_bird.jpg/340px-Male_Frigate_bird.jpg 2x" data-file-width="399" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Frigatebird" title="Frigatebird">Frigatebird</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Avifauna in the wild, both native and migrating include <a href="/wiki/Brown_pelican" title="Brown pelican">brown pelican</a> along the shoreline, <a href="/wiki/Magnificent_frigatebird" title="Magnificent frigatebird">magnificent frigatebirds</a> with long wingspans of up to 1.8 metres (6'), <a href="/wiki/Green_heron" title="Green heron">green herons</a>, <a href="/wiki/Snowy_egret" title="Snowy egret">snowy egrets</a>, <a href="/wiki/Belted_kingfisher" title="Belted kingfisher">belted kingfishers</a>; <a href="/wiki/Bananaquit" title="Bananaquit">bananaquits</a>; <a href="/wiki/Broad-winged_hawk" title="Broad-winged hawk">broad-winged hawks</a>; two species of <a href="/wiki/Hummingbird" title="Hummingbird">hummingbirds</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Green-throated_carib" title="Green-throated carib">green-throated carib</a> and <a href="/wiki/Antillean_crested_hummingbird" title="Antillean crested hummingbird">Antillean crested hummingbird</a>; and <a href="/wiki/Zenaida_doves" title="Zenaida doves">zenaida doves</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.12-13_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.12-13-67">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Aquafauna">Aquafauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Aquafauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ocypode_quadrata_(Cahuita).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Ocypode_quadrata_%28Cahuita%29.jpg/220px-Ocypode_quadrata_%28Cahuita%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Ocypode_quadrata_%28Cahuita%29.jpg/330px-Ocypode_quadrata_%28Cahuita%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Ocypode_quadrata_%28Cahuita%29.jpg/440px-Ocypode_quadrata_%28Cahuita%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3798" data-file-height="2848" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Ghost_crab" title="Ghost crab">Ghost crab</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The marine life found here consists of <a href="/wiki/Anemones" class="mw-redirect" title="Anemones">anemones</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sea_urchin" title="Sea urchin">urchins</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sea_cucumbers" class="mw-redirect" title="Sea cucumbers">sea cucumbers</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Eels" class="mw-redirect" title="Eels">eels</a>, which all live on the reefs along with <a href="/wiki/Turtle" title="Turtle">turtles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Conch" title="Conch">conch</a> and many varieties of marine fishes.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.181_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.181-68">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The marine aquafauna is rich in conch, which has pearly-pink shells. Its meat is a favourite <a href="/wiki/Food_supplement" class="mw-redirect" title="Food supplement">food supplement</a> item and their shells are a collector's item. Other species of fish that are recorded close to the shoreline in shallow waters are: <a href="/wiki/Sergeant_major_(fish)" title="Sergeant major (fish)">sergeant majors</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Chromis_cyanea" title="Chromis cyanea">blue chromis</a>, brown <a href="/wiki/Chromis" title="Chromis">chromis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Surgeon_fish" class="mw-redirect" title="Surgeon fish">surgeon fish</a>; blue <a href="/wiki/Tang_(fish)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tang (fish)">tangs</a> and <a href="/wiki/Trumpet_fish" class="mw-redirect" title="Trumpet fish">trumpet fish</a>. On the shore are <a href="/wiki/Ghost_crabs" class="mw-redirect" title="Ghost crabs">ghost crabs</a>, which always live on the beach in small burrowed tunnels made of sand, and the <a href="/wiki/Hermit_crabs" class="mw-redirect" title="Hermit crabs">hermit crabs</a>, which live on land but lay eggs in water and which also eat garbage and sewerage. They spend some months in the sea during and after the hatching season.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.13-14_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.13-14-69">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Marine_Reserve">Marine Reserve</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Marine Reserve">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Saint-Barthélemy has a marine nature reserve, known as the Reserve Naturelle<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> that covers 1200 ha (4¾ sq.mi.), and is divided into 5 zones all around the island to form a network of protected areas. The Reserve includes the bays of Grand Cul de Sac, Colombier, Marigot, Petit Cul de Sac, and Petite Anse as well as waters around offshore rocks such as Les Gross Islets, Pain de Sucre, Tortue, and Forchue. The Reserve is designed to protect the islands' coral reefs, seagrass, and endangered marine species including sea turtles. The Reserve has two levels of protection, the yellow zones of protection where certain non-extractive activities, like snorkeling and boating, are allowed and the red zones of high protection where most activities including SCUBA are restricted to protect or recover marine life. Anchoring is prohibited in the Reserve and mooring buoys are in place in some of the protected bays like Colombier.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Landmarks_and_architecture">Landmarks and architecture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Landmarks and architecture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>As well as Gustavia, the capital of St. Barthélemy, there are many notable places and monuments on the island which testify to the island's colonial history under the Spanish, Swedish, British, and French, and now a French territory.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.170-173_33-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.170-173-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Gustavia">Gustavia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Gustavia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Gustavia_Harbor,_Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Gustavia_Harbor%2C_Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.jpg/220px-Gustavia_Harbor%2C_Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Gustavia_Harbor%2C_Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.jpg/330px-Gustavia_Harbor%2C_Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Gustavia_Harbor%2C_Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.jpg/440px-Gustavia_Harbor%2C_Saint-Barth%C3%A9lemy.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="960" /></a><figcaption>Gustavia Harbour</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Gustavia,_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Gustavia, Saint Barthélemy">Gustavia</a> is in a U-shaped cove facing the harbour on the west. The coastal arm of this cove is in a <a href="/wiki/Peninsula" title="Peninsula">peninsula</a> while the dockyard is on the east side.
</p><p>When the British invaded the harbour town in 1744<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>, the town's architectural buildings were destroyed.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Subsequently, new structures were built in the town around the harbour area<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> and the Swedes had also further added to the architectural beauty of the town in 1785 with more buildings, when they had occupied the town. Earlier to their occupation, the port was known as "Carénage". The Swedes renamed it as Gustavia in honour of their king <a href="/wiki/Gustav_III" title="Gustav III">Gustav III</a>. It was then their prime trading center. The port maintained a neutral stance since the Caribbean war was on in the 18th century. They used it as a trading post of contraband and the city of <a href="/wiki/Gustavia,_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Gustavia, Saint Barthélemy">Gustavia</a> prospered but this prosperity was short-lived.<sup id="cite_ref-Introduction_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Introduction-28">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>These buildings also underwent further destruction during the hurricanes and also by gutting in 1852. However, some monuments are still intact such as the residence of the then Swedish governor, now the town hall<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>. The oldest colonial structure in the town is stated to be the bell tower (now without a bell) built in 1799, as part of a church (destroyed in the past), in the southeast end of the town on Rue Du Presbytere. Now, a large clock is installed in place of the bell.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.170-173_33-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.170-173-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The road that runs parallel to the harbour face of the sea is called the Rue de la Republique and two other roads connect to the two arms of the U-shaped bay. The city has a network of roads, inherited from the Swedish period, that are laid in a grid pattern, which are either parallel or perpendicular to the three main roads that encompass the bay.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.170_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.170-71">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id=".C3.89glise_anglicane_de_Gustavia"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Église_anglicane_de_Gustavia">Église anglicane de Gustavia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Église anglicane de Gustavia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Église anglicane de Gustavia, the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint-Bartholomew_Anglican_Church&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Saint-Bartholomew Anglican Church (page does not exist)">Saint-Bartholomew Anglican Church</a>, is an important religious building in the town built in 1855 with stones brought from St Eustatius. It is on one of the town's most elegant roads, called the Rue du Centenaire. It has a bell tower. A rock wall encircles the church.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.170-173_33-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.170-173-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Ancien_presbyt.C3.A8re_de_l.27.C3.A9glise_catholique_de_Gustavia"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Ancien_presbytère_de_l'église_catholique_de_Gustavia">Ancien presbytère de l'église catholique de Gustavia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Ancien presbytère de l&#039;église catholique de Gustavia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Ancien presbytère de l'église catholique de Gustavia is the Catholic Church built in 1822 and is a replacement of the oldest church of the same name in Lorient.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> This church also has a bell tower which is separated from the main church and which rings loud and clear.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.170-173_33-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.170-173-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Mus.C3.A9e_Territorial_de_St.-Barth.C3.A9lemy"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Musée_Territorial_de_St.-Barthélemy">Musée Territorial de St.-Barthélemy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Musée Territorial de St.-Barthélemy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Musée Territorial de St.-Barthélemy is a historical museum known as the "St. Barts Municipal Museum" also called the "Wall House" (musée – bibliothèque) in Gustavia, which is located on the far end of La Pointe. The museum is housed in an old stone house, a two-story building that has been refurbished. The island's history relating to the French, Swedish and British periods of occupation is well presented in the museum with photographs, maps, and paintings. Also on display are ancestral costumes, antique tools, models of <a href="/wiki/Creole_peoples" title="Creole peoples">Creole</a> houses, and ancient fishing boats. It also houses a library.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.170-173_33-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.170-173-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-island_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-island-72">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Gustavia_Lighthouse">Gustavia Lighthouse</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Gustavia Lighthouse">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Gustaviaphare2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Gustaviaphare2.jpg/170px-Gustaviaphare2.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="256" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Gustaviaphare2.jpg/255px-Gustaviaphare2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Gustaviaphare2.jpg/340px-Gustaviaphare2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="851" data-file-height="1280" /></a><figcaption>Gustavia Lighthouse</figcaption></figure>
<p>The 9 metres (30&#160;ft) white tower of the Gustavia Lighthouse was built in 1961. Situated on the crest of a hill north of the town, its focal plane is 64 metres (210&#160;ft) above the level of the sea.  It flashes every 12 seconds, white, green, or red depending on direction. The round conical tower has a single red band at the top.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Forts">Forts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Forts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Among the notable structures in the town are the three forts built by the Swedes for defense purposes. One of these forts, known as Fort Oscar (formerly Gustav Adolph), which overlooks the sea is located on the far side of La Pointe. However, the ruins have been replaced by a modern military building which now houses the local gendarmerie. The other fort known as Fort Karl now presents very few ruins. The third fort built by the Swedes is Fort Gustav, which is also seen in ruins strewn around the weather station and the Light House. The fort built in 1787 over a hill slope has ruins of ramparts, a guardhouse, a munitions depot, a wood-burning oven, and so forth.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.170-173_33-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.170-173-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-island_72-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-island-72">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Savaku">Savaku</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Savaku">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>A statue, "Savaku", representing the <a href="/wiki/Arawak" title="Arawak">Arawak</a> peoples is present at Saint-Jean.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The island's public preschools and primary schools, under the authority of the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Acad%C3%A9mie_de_la_Guadeloupe&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Académie de la Guadeloupe (page does not exist)">Académie de la Guadeloupe</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acad%C3%A9mie_de_la_Guadeloupe" class="extiw" title="fr:Académie de la Guadeloupe">fr</a>&#93;</span>, are <sup id="cite_ref-PSlist_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PSlist-75">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>École primaire Gustavia</li>
<li>École maternelle Gustavia</li></ul>
<p>Private primary schools:<sup id="cite_ref-PSlist_75-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PSlist-75">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>École primaire privée Saint Joseph</li>
<li>École primaire privée Sainte Marie</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Festivals_and_holidays">Festivals and holidays</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Festivals and holidays">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Some of the festivals held each year in St. Barthélemy are:
</p>
<ul><li>The St. Barts Music Festival is held every January, usually during the 2nd and 3rd weeks.</li>
<li>A French Carnival in February / March held for two weeks before <a href="/wiki/Ash_Wednesday" title="Ash Wednesday">Ash Wednesday</a> and concluding with Ash Wednesday; on Ash Wednesday a black and white parade held at Shell Beach is the occasion of a notional burning of the image of Vaval, the Carnival King.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St._Barth_Film_Festival" title="St. Barth Film Festival">St. Barth Film Festival</a>, held annually at the end of April, was established in 1996, and hosts Caribbean films for five days.<sup id="cite_ref-Flippin2012_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Flippin2012-76">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Armistice Day on 8 May.</li>
<li>Abolition of Slavery Day on 27 May and 9 October.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Bastille_Day" title="Bastille Day">Bastille Day</a> on 14 July.</li>
<li>Victor Schoelcher Day on 21 July honouring Schoelcher, a French parliamentarian for his noble humanitarian act of abolishing slavery in French territory on 27 April 1848.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Assumption_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="Assumption Day">Assumption Day</a> on 15 August.</li>
<li>Fête de Saint Barthélemy feast day of Saint Barthélemy on 24 August, in honour of the island's patron saint. Church bells are rung, boats are blessed and a regatta is held, followed by fireworks and a public ball.</li>
<li>Festival of Gustavia held in August, an occasion of dragnet fishing and partying.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/All_Saints_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="All Saints Day">All Saints Day</a> on 1 November</li>
<li>Remembrance Day (Armistice Day).</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Christmas_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="Christmas Day">Christmas Day</a> on 25 December; and <a href="/wiki/New_Year%27s_Eve" title="New Year&#39;s Eve">New Year's Eve</a> on 31 December.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.173_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.173-77">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>Some other festivals held are the Festival Gastronomique (April) and Yacht Festival (May).<sup id="cite_ref-Henderson2005_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Henderson2005-78">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> The national holidays observed are the Bastille Day and St. Barthélemy Day (day of adoption of <a href="/wiki/French_Constitution" class="mw-redirect" title="French Constitution">French Constitution</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-Government_10-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Government-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/w/index.php?title=Feast_of_St_Louis&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Feast of St Louis (page does not exist)">Feast of St Louis</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> is held on 1 November when thousands of candles are lit in the evening hours, which is a public holiday. <a href="/wiki/All_Souls_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="All Souls Day">All Souls Day</a> is observed on 2 November, and it is a public holiday.<sup id="cite_ref-Cameron,_pp.588_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cameron,_pp.588-79">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Caribbean, the birthplace of the <a href="/wiki/Calypso_music" title="Calypso music">calypso</a>, <a href="/wiki/M%C3%A9ringue" title="Méringue">méringue</a>, <a href="/wiki/Soca_music" title="Soca music">soca</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zouk_(musical_movement)" class="mw-redirect" title="Zouk (musical movement)">zouk</a> and <a href="/wiki/Reggae" title="Reggae">reggae</a> music influence the culture tremendously.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.18_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.18-80">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> The St. Barthélemy Music Festival<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> is a major international performing arts event held every year.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cinema">Cinema</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Cinema">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Film director <a href="/wiki/Dylan_Verrechia" title="Dylan Verrechia">Dylan Verrechia</a> is originally from St. Barthélemy.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/French_cuisine" title="French cuisine">French cuisine</a>, <a href="/wiki/West_Indian_cuisine" class="mw-redirect" title="West Indian cuisine">West Indian cuisine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Creole_cuisine" title="Creole cuisine">Creole cuisine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Italian_cuisine" title="Italian cuisine">Italian cuisine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Asian_cuisine" title="Asian cuisine">Asian cuisine</a> are common in St. Barthélemy. The island has over 70 restaurants serving many dishes and others are a significant number of gourmet restaurants; many of the finest restaurants are located in the hotels.<sup id="cite_ref-Cameron,_pp.586–587_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cameron,_pp.586–587-82">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> There are also several snack restaurants which the French call "les snacks" or "les petits creux" which include sandwiches, pizzas, and salads.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.206_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.206-83">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> In West Indian cuisine, steamed vegetables with fresh fish is common; Creole dishes tend to be spicier.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.206_83-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.206-83">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> The island hosts gastronomic events throughout the year, with dishes such as spring roll of shrimp and bacon, fresh grilled <a href="/wiki/Lobster" title="Lobster">lobster</a>, Chinese noodle salad with coconut milk, and grilled beef fillet, etc.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Restaurants such as Maya's which serves Creole dishes and L'Isola which serves Italian are some of the most popular restaurants on the island. Maya's also has a "to go" store where you can take out food on the beach or just take it home. <sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the early 1990s, the island had two cooking schools:  the Saint Barts Cooking School which emphasizes classical French cuisine and Cooking in Paradise which emphasizes creole cuisine.<sup id="cite_ref-Harris1991_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Harris1991-86">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Fashion">Fashion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Fashion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The traditional costume which is seen only among older women consists of starched white bonnets called <i>kichnottes</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Cameron(2007)_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cameron(2007)-61">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Legend">Legend</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Legend">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>A popular legend related to St. Barthélemy is of a seafarer hooligan looking to loot Spanish ships. French pirate <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Montbars" title="Daniel Montbars">Daniel Montbars</a>, who was given the epithet "Montbars the Exterminator", took shelter in St. Barthélemy during his pirate operations and hid the loot in the sandy coves at Anse du Gouverneur.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.22_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.22-87">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union_in_Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy" title="Rugby union in Saint Barthélemy">Rugby union in Saint Barthélemy</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_para_surfer.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_para_surfer.jpg/170px-St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_para_surfer.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_para_surfer.jpg/255px-St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_para_surfer.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_para_surfer.jpg/340px-St._Barts_Baie_de_Saint-Jean_para_surfer.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="1600" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Kitesurfing" class="mw-redirect" title="Kitesurfing">Kitesurfing</a> at Baie de Saint-Jean</figcaption></figure>
<p>Rugby is a popular sport on the island. One of the major teams on the island is "Les Barracudas," named after the ferocious fish of the Caribbean. They often play teams from <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union_in_Anguilla" title="Rugby union in Anguilla">Anguilla</a> and other surrounding islands.<sup id="cite_ref-Anguillan_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Anguillan-88">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gustavia is also known as a haven for yachting, with many events being held there each year. These include the <a href="/wiki/St_Barths_Bucket_Regatta" title="St Barths Bucket Regatta">St Barths Bucket Regatta</a>, the Saint Barth's Cup and Les Voiles de St. Barth in April, and the International Regatta in May.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> Deep sea fishing is also undertaken from the waterfront of Lorient, Flamands, and Corossol to fish for <a href="/wiki/Tuna" title="Tuna">tuna</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marlin" title="Marlin">marlin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bonito" title="Bonito">bonito</a>, <a href="/wiki/Barracuda" title="Barracuda">barracuda</a> and <a href="/wiki/Wahoo" title="Wahoo">wahoo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.180_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.180-90">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> St Barth Open Fishing tournament is held in July.<sup id="cite_ref-Cameron,_pp.588_79-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cameron,_pp.588-79">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Transat AG2R Race, held every alternate year, is an event that originates in Concarneau in Brittany, France, reaching St. Barthélemy. It is a boat race with boats of 10-metre (33-foot) length with a single hull and with essential safety equipment. Each boat is navigated by two sailors. <a href="/wiki/Kitesurfing" class="mw-redirect" title="Kitesurfing">Kitesurfing</a> and other water sports have also become popular on the island in recent years, especially at Grand Cul-de-Sac beach (Baie de Grand Cul de Sac) for windy sports as kitesurfing and Saint Jean Beach ( Baie de Saint Jean), Lorient, Toiny and Anse des Cayes for surfing. Tennis is also popular on the island and it has several tennis clubs, Tennis Clube de Flamboyant in Grand Cul-de-Sac, AJOE Tennis Club in Orient, and ASCO in Colombier.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.183_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.183-91">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Swedish Marathon Race, also called the <i>Gustavialoppet</i>, is held in December. Races of 2 kilometres (1.2&#160;mi) and 12 kilometres (7.5&#160;mi) are conducted when children, women, and men participate in the races.<sup id="cite_ref-Cameron,_pp.588_79-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cameron,_pp.588-79">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Barts_private_boat_docked.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/St._Barts_private_boat_docked.jpg/220px-St._Barts_private_boat_docked.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/St._Barts_private_boat_docked.jpg/330px-St._Barts_private_boat_docked.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/St._Barts_private_boat_docked.jpg/440px-St._Barts_private_boat_docked.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption>Private boat docked in St. Barts</figcaption></figure>
<p>Aeroport Saint Barthélemy Remy de Haenen (<a href="/wiki/Gustaf_III_Airport" title="Gustaf III Airport">Gustaf III Airport</a>) is small with a runway length of 646 meter/2,119 ft. Airport codes: SBH (IATA), TFFJ (ICAO). The airport is served by regional commercial aircraft and charters of up to 19 passengers, as well as helicopters.
In 2022 the airport recorded 218,984 passengers and 44,521 aircraft movements. A traffic peak of 280 aircraft movements per day was recorded on January 2, 2022, and 323 aircraft on January 2, 2023.
58% of the passengers for destination St. Barth arrive on international commercial airlines and large private jets mainly via the neighboring island <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten" title="Sint Maarten">Sint Maarten</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Princess_Juliana_International_Airport" title="Princess Juliana International Airport">Princess Juliana International Airport</a>. 15% arrive via San Juan, Puerto Rico, 13&#160;% via Guadeloupe. 
St. Barth has its own airline, St. Barth Commuter, which in addition to the scheduled and charter flight services, provides medical transport services. St. Barth Executive is a local aircraft charter operator,
</p><p>Many inter-island ferry services operate regularly between St. Martin and St. Barts.<sup id="cite_ref-Sullivan,_p.160_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sullivan,_p.160-92">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> There are three ferry services active at the moment, one is operated by Voyager (which leaves from <a href="/wiki/Marigot,_Saint_Martin" title="Marigot, Saint Martin">Marigot</a>) one day-trip ferry by the Edge (Leaving from Simpsons Bay) and one regular ferry service by the Great Bay Ferry from <a href="/wiki/Philipsburg,_Sint_Maarten" title="Philipsburg, Sint Maarten">Philipsburg</a>. 
</p><p>The narrow and congested roads, and difficulty in parking, have been an impetus for driving <a href="/wiki/Smart_(automobile)" class="mw-redirect" title="Smart (automobile)">Smart cars</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-KohnLandon2006_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KohnLandon2006-93">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag needs to be fact-checked with the cited source(s). (January 2011)">verification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>A weekly journal entitled <i>Journal de St. Barth</i> is published in the <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French language</a>. Its <a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English-language</a> abridged version, <i>St. Barth Weekly</i>, is published only during the winter (for Anglophone tourists). Reflecting the island's popularity with the rich and famous, the high-fashion magazine <i><a href="/wiki/L%27Officiel" title="L&#39;Officiel">L'Officiel</a></i> publishes a seasonal local edition. Other tourist-related information is available at the airport and in the offices of the Tourist Authority.<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are two local TV broadcasters and five FM radio channels (2021). The island has a fully integrated access telephone system with capability for the direct dialing on fixed and wireless systems. There is a 4G/LTE mobile service since 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-Communications_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Communications-94">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Health_facilities">Health facilities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Health facilities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The island has a small hospital, the Hôpital de Bruyn, in Gustavia with an adjacent diagnostic laboratory. There is also at least one private diagnostic facility.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Specialists in cardiology, general medicine, dentists, ENT, OB/GYN, <a href="/wiki/Paediatrics" class="mw-redirect" title="Paediatrics">paediatrics</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rheumatology" title="Rheumatology">rheumatology</a> are also available. There are many pharmacies dispensing medicines. For more advanced facilities, patients go to Guadeloupe, <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>, <a href="/wiki/San_Juan,_Puerto_Rico" title="San Juan, Puerto Rico">San Juan</a> or France.<sup id="cite_ref-Nash2008_27-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Nash2008-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_people">Notable people</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Notable people">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Eug%C3%A9nie_Blanchard" class="mw-redirect" title="Eugénie Blanchard">Eugénie Blanchard</a> was the world's oldest living person (114 years, 261 days) at the time of her death on 4 November 2010. She was born on St. Barthélemy and spent most of her life on <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Curaçao">Curaçao</a> and St. Barthélemy as a Catholic nun.<sup id="cite_ref-latimes_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-latimes-96">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Johnny_Hallyday" title="Johnny Hallyday">Johnny Hallyday</a> chose to be buried on St. Barthélemy in the <a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%89glise_Notre-Dame_de_l%27Assomption,_Lorient&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Église Notre-Dame de l&#39;Assomption, Lorient (page does not exist)"><i>Église de Lorient</i></a> parish cemetery.</li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Barth%C3%A9lemy&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-2"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-2">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">25 km² including the outlying uninhabited islets. 21 km² without the outlying islets.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-5"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-5">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Last population census in January 2017. The next population census in Saint Barthélemy will take place in January 2023.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-12"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-12">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">25 km² including the outlying uninhabited islets. 21 km² without the outlying islets.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-16"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-16">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">There currently is not enough archeological evidence to give a more detailed description about the pre-Columbian presence.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-35"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-35">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Last population census in January 2017. The next population census in Saint Barthélemy will take place in January 2023.<a rel="nofollow" class="external autonumber" href="https://www.journaldesaintbarth.com/actualites/vie-locale/le-recensement-repousse-a-2023-202201281531.html">[1]</a></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
